## Paper Source: Understanding the learning of disabled students An exploration of machine learning approaches

## Understanding the learning of disabled students: An exploration of machine learning approaches

Maliha Homaira Faculty of Engineering &amp; Information Technology University of Technology Sydney

Sydney, Australia maliha.homaira@student.uts.edu.au

Amara Atif Faculty of Engineering &amp; Information Technology University of Technology Sydney Sydney, Australia amara.atif@uts.edu.au

Abstract -Research  is  limited  in  demonstrating  how  learning analytics can support students with a learning disability. It is vital to scrutinise the demographics of students with and without disabilities and  their  effect  on  performance.  Machine  learning  algorithms  can provide valuable insights by  mining  this  expanding  education data. This study aims to analyse the relationship between disabled and nondisabled students' demographic with their scores and the number of attempts to complete a module to develop prediction models. Three models - Adaptive Boosting, Random Forest, and K nearest neighbour -  were used. The results found that the Adaptive boosting algorithm delivered the highest prediction accuracy.

Lisa-Angelique Lim Connected Intelligence Centre

University of Technology Sydney Sydney, Australia lisa-angelique.lim@uts.edu.au

Moreover, the accuracy of predictive models is often in question [19]. In this paper, we take a first step toward developing a fair model for predicting students' on a publicly available dataset, Open University Learning Analytics Dataset (OULAD), Open University,  as  we  could  not  find  any  dataset  available  in  the Australian context. This paper uses three prediction models to identify relationships between the demographic factor (disabled and non-disabled students) scores on different assessments. The results from our exploratory analysis provide an initial insight into the learning of students with learning disabilities.

KeywordsLearning Analytics, Learning Disability, Demographic, Prediction, Adaboost, Random Forest, kNN, Machine Learning

## I. INTRODUCTION

As technology is developing at a tremendous pace, the use of technology in learning has also developed over time. Along with this, Educational Data Mining (EDM) advancements have seen the rise of learning analytics (LA) as a related field [20]. These  developments  make  it  plausible to identify at-risk students, predict their scores, build adaptive learning environments, and facilitate personalised feedback. It enables to development of the quality of education [22].

Despite  these  developments,  little  consideration  has  been given  to  students  with  learning  disabilities  [11].  The  term "learning disability" is interpreted differently in different countries.  In  Australia,  the  term  learning  disability  refers  to students with severe difficulty in literacy and numeracy; most of the time, learning disabilities are regarded to have a neurological basis [14]. Dyslexia, dysgraphia, and dyscalculia are a few of the learning disabilities. In  most  cases,  students  do  not  feel  it safe or necessary to disclose their disability [13]. At the same time,  learning-disabled  students  suffer  from  issues  that  can impede their learning, such as stress and anxiety, lack of selfesteem, poor study-life balance, personal relationship problems, and financial pressures [16]. Therefore, there is an urgent need to  identify  students'  learning  disabilities  in  a  way  that  allows them  to  feel  safe  and  support  their  learning  in  response  to specific disabilities.

For this purpose, developing a fair machine learning model is crucial; [19], however, such models are sparse in the literature.

## II. LITERATURE REVIEW

In  this  section,  we  will  discuss  the  difficulties  faced  by students  with  learning  disabilities  and  the  machine  learning approaches that have been taken to acknowledge and resolve these issues.

## A. Learning Disability

Conventional  teaching  practices  in  physical  and  online learning environments fail to consider students  with learning disabilities, which creates significant issues for these students. As a result, students with dyscalculia - difficulty with counting, dyslexia - and students with difficulty in reading some specific fonts  are  left  behind  [21].  The  rapid  digitisation  of  learning technology has failed to consider the compatibility of current education practices with disabled students compared to students who do not have such challenges. For example, while uploading lectures online, not having a text description or accessibility in reading-specific fonts can add more challenges to learning for learning-disabled  students  [11].  As  a  result  of  not  receiving enough support, children face difficulties throughout adulthood and  perform  less  than  optimally  at  universities.  Additional consequences can occur when students take anti-depressant or anti-anxiety medication [1]. Learning analytics offers a promising  way  to  support  these  students  by  drawing  on individual  learning  data  and  providing  timely  interventions. However,  for  this to happen,  practical machine  learning approaches are needed.

## B. Machine Learning Approaches

With advances in EDM and LA, various machine learning approaches have been widely employed [3]. Three approaches have  been  discussed  in  the  literature.  The  random  forest algorithm  has  proved  to  be  more  than  81%  accurate  in predicting students' performance using the OULAD dataset [9]. The random forest can be used in batch and adaptive modes for predicting students' success with the expanding education data. AdaBoost has delivered promising results of 79% at the end of the first semester and increased its accuracy to 90% at the end of  the  semester  [10].  Using  multilayer  perception,  AdaBoost has 87.6% accuracy in classifying and predicting the performance  of  students  enrolled  in  software  engineering courses  [4].  Anxiety  prediction,  with  an  accuracy  of  0.943, among  the students was performed using the  AdaBoost machine learning model [6]. Although KNN has not provided promising accuracy in predicting student performance, it can be used  as  a  classification  algorithm,  and  the  accuracy  varies depending on the dataset [7].

## III. RESEARCH QUESTIONS

This study explores the possibilities of a machine learning approach to support students with disabilities. Three research questions guided this study:

- RQ 1: Is there any relationship between disabled and non-disabled students' demographics and their scores in different assessment types?
- RQ 2: Is there any relationship between disabled and non-disabled students' demographics with the number of attempts they take to complete a module?
- RQ  3:  Is  there a difference in the  accuracy  of predictions made by different machine learning algorithms?

## IV. METHODOLOGY

In  this  paper,  we  focused  on  three  algorithms,  Adaboost, Random  Forest,  and  kNN,  and  compared  their  prediction accuracy with MSE, RMSE, MAE, and R-squared values. The model-building  process  was  performed  in  the  Orange  Data Mining application. The models were developed in five steps. Starting from step 1 Data Collection, exploratory data analysis &amp;  Data preparation, selection &amp;  training of the model, calculating model accuracy, and lastly analysing and delivering the results fig[1].

Fig 1: Model Development Stages



## A. Dataset

The dataset for this study was an Open University Learning Analytics  Dataset  (OULAD)  developed  by  Open  University from their virtual learning courses in 2013 and 2014. The most valuable fact of this dataset is that it contains the demographics of 32,593 students and their outcomes in 22 courses, including the  logs  of  their  interactions  [15].  For  this  study,  only  the following variables were considered from the dataset: demographics  (age,  gender,  highest  education,  disability);  a number  of  previous  attempts;  final  results  and  assessments; assessment score; assessment type (Computer Marked Assessment  (CMA),  exam,  and  Tutor  Marked  Assessment (TMA)). The demographics were extracted from the studentInfo table,  which  contained  32,593  rows.  The  studentAssessment table contained 173,912 records [15].

## B. Data Preparation

Data pre-processing was conducted in Python, while visualisations  were  performed  in  Tableau.  Outliers  and  any presence  of  N/A  values  were  checked  in  all  three  tables.  In order  to  answer  our  research  questions,  we  divided  the  table into 1) details of students with / had declared disability; and 2) details of students without / had not declared any disability. To investigate  relationships  between  the  disability  factor  and scores  in  different  assessment  types  and  on  a  number  of previous  attempts,  the  gender  and  highest  education  of  the disabled and non-disabled students were analysed at the next stage using exploratory data analysis.

## C. Visualisations

On  the  other  hand,  male  non-disabled students scored more in TMA 32.11%. From fig [2] and [3], female disabled students scored higher in the assessments than male disabled students. Contrastingly male non-disabled students  were  visualised  to  perform  better  than  the  female students. This visualisation supports the relationship between disability and gender scores in different assessments.

## D. Adaptive Boost Algorithm (AdaBoost)

Adaptive Boosting or AdaBoost, one of the most effective ensemble  learning  algorithms,  works  by  aggregating  all  the weak classifiers, generating a strong one [8] and sustaining an adaptive  weight  distribution  [18].  A  weighted  accumulation that represents the outcomes of the weighted classifier is linked with  the  outputs  of  the  weak  students.  It  works  both  for classification and regression. The algorithm,



every is a classifier, weak student, that outputs the predicted class in relation to input x. Weak students produce prediction outputs, t = for every iteration Et = sum of training error.



In this case, E(F) is the error function. AdaBoost tweaks the weak students and improves the individual results.

## E. Random Forest Algorithm

Random forest, another ensemble classification technique to  enhance  classification  accuracy,  generates  a  number  of decision trees that are trained on various portions of the same training  set  [5].  To  create  the  K  number,  a  random  forest randomly chooses the attributes. The error rate determines the strength of the tree; the stronger the tree, the stronger the forest will  be.  Random  Forest  can  handle  categorical  variables  and missing  values,  works  effectively  on  large  datasets,  do  not require pruning, and never overfits [2]. Random forest takes 2/3 of  the  original  data,  and  the  rest  are  used  for  classifying unbiased errors, called out-of-bag (oob). Gini Index is used to determine the final class in each tree. Each class's Gini index is determined by subtracting the sum of squared probabilities. As defined in [5], the Gini index equation is,



## F. K Nearest Neighbour Algorithm (kNN)

Out  of  all  the  machine  learning  algorithms,  kNN  is  the simplest,  having  several  uses  in  various  industries,  including pattern recognition, online  marketing, and cluster analysis of image datasets. The k-value specifies how many neighbours are closest. The neighbours are chosen from a group of objects or objects with similar characteristics or values [17]. One advantage of kNN is that it is easy to understand and has a fasttraining rate [7]. Euclidean's distance is calculated to calculate the length as given [17],



## G. Performance Measures

The essential part of developing a machine learning model is determining its accuracy. Mean Squared Error (MSE), Mean Absolute Error (MAE), Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE), and R squared were used to determine the accuracy of the model's prediction.  MSE  illustrates  the  average  squared  difference between the dataset's original value and the model's predicted value. RMSE is simply the square root of MSE. MAE, on the other hand, measures the average of the residuals. R-squared represents the proportion of the variance. The lesser the MSE, RMSE, and MAE value, the more accurate the model. A higher R-squared  value  represents  a  better  model  for  the  particular dataset, which usually falls between 0 - 1. The mathematical background is calculated as defined in [12]:











## V. ANALYSIS

## A. RQ 3: Prediction Accuracy of the algorithms

1) Disabled  Students: AdaBoost  produced  the  highest accuracy  for  both  gender  and  demographic  vs  score  dataset. From Table 1, for gender vs score, the R squared value was the highest in the AdaBoost algorithm, 0.998. The MSE, RMSE, and MAE values were also the lowest, 0.739 MSE, 0.204 for RMSE, and 0.204 for MAE for this algorithm. For the higher education vs score dataset, the AdaBoost model provided the highest  R  squared  value,  0.998  and  the  lowest  MSE:  0.749, RMSE: 0.865,  and  MAE:  0.211.  For  the  other  two  datasets, gender vs a number of previous attempts and highest education vs a number of previous attempts, random forest showed the highest  accuracy.  In  gender  vs  number  of  previous  attempts' accuracy,  the  random  forest  has  the  highest  R  squared  value among the other models, 0.787. This model also has the least MSE:  0.067,  RMSE:  0.260,  and  MAE:  0.111.  In  highest education vs number of attempts, the random forest provided the leading R squared value, 0.789. This model has also lowest MSE:  0.067,  RMSE:  0.259,  and  MAE:  0.111  values.  Both AdaBoost and Random Forest models have illustrated satisfactory accuracy. kNN on the other hand demonstrated the lowest accuracy.

## VI. DISCUSSION

The analysis of the OULAD dataset found no significant difference between disabled students' and non-disabled students' performance. However, gender and highest education demographics revealed a significant difference between these two demographic students.

It  is  necessary  to  scrutinise  every  aspect  to  have  a  clear visualisation of the factors that may affect the performance of disabled students. The number of previous attempts taken was higher for disabled students, and the highest level of education in  disabled  students  was  only  "lower  than  A  level"  for  most students. On the other hand, the highest level of education for non-disabled students was "A level or equivalent", fig [7]. This demonstrates the need to emphasise more support for inclusive education. Although the percentage of total scores in different assessment  types  was  not  much  difference  between  disabled and  non-disabled  students,  a  significant  difference  in  scores were observed  when  gender was considered. This study also found  that  different  machine  learning  approaches  yielded different results for prediction. In this case, Adaboost showed the  highest  accuracy  for  both  datasets,  followed  by  random forest.  kNN  reported  the  lowest  accuracy  for  our  dataset. Nevertheless, as the model's accuracy depends on datasets and actions, kNN can still be used in other prediction models.

## VII. CONCLUSION &amp; FUTURE WORK

More  thorough  research  is  needed  to  analyse  "learning disability"  in  higher  education.  As  this  was  an  exploratory study,  we  acknowledge  the  possible  limitations.  Firstly,  this study employed a smaller number of demographic and score data.  Secondly,  the  dataset  did  not  elaborate  on  the  type  of disabilities students had. Thirdly, there were limitations of the prediction models. Lastly, we did not have publicly available dataset of  Australian learners  with learning disability. Future work  will  firstly  include  applying  adaptive  learning  models with  more  data  to  acquire  better  results  and  secondly,  more emphasize will be given on analysisng the Australian context with accurate data for learninig disability.

The authors would like to thank all the family members who cooperated during this research and  Univeristy of Technology Sydney for giving us the opportunity to work together.

## Computers and Education: Artificial Intelligence

## Understanding the role of study strategies and learning disabilities on student academic performance to enhance educational approaches: A proposal using artificial intelligence

Adriano Bressane a,b,* , Daniel Zwirn a , Alexei Essiptchouk a , Ant ˆ onio Carlos Varela Saraiva a , Fernando Luiz de Campos Carvalho a , Jorge Kennety Silva Formiga a , Líliam C ´ esar de Castro Medeiros a , Rog ´ erio Galante Negri a,c

## A R T I C L E  I N F O

Keywords: Study strategies Learning limitation and potential Educational approach

## 1. Introduction

How can study strategies integrated into artificial intelligence (AI) tools mitigate the impact of learning disabilities on academic performance?  Achieving  optimal  academic  performance  is  a  multifaceted challenge that requires a profound understanding of study strategies and their  interaction  with  learning  disabilities.  The  pursuit  of  effective educational approaches that cater to the diverse needs of students remains an ongoing endeavor, marked by the ever-evolving integration of

## A B S T R A C T

Statement of problem: The students ' academic performance is influenced by a complex interplay among several factors. Traditional educational approaches often struggle to accommodate the diverse needs of students, leading to suboptimal learning outcomes.

Purpose: This article aims to comprehensively understand the role of study strategies and learning disabilities in shaping academic performance. Through the integration of artificial intelligence (AI) tools, the purpose is to propose a decision support system (DSS) for recommendations to improve the educational approach.

Method: To identify features with higher explanatory power based on empirical data, we employed an artificial neural network (ANN) to recognize patterns of association between study strategies, learning disabilities, and academic performance. Using the pondered features, a Fuzzy-based AI was built for offering recommendations into effective educational interventions.

Conclusions: The findings underscore the significance of study strategies in mitigating the negative impact of learning disabilities on academic performance. By leveraging the proposed AI tools framework, educators can make informed decisions to tailor educational approaches, catering to the unique cognitive profiles of students. Personalized interventions based on identified patterns can lead to improved academic outcomes and greater inclusivity in the learning environment.

Practical  implications: Educators  and  policymakers  can  adopt  the  proposed  data-driven  strategies  to  enhance teaching methodologies, thereby accommodating the varying needs of students with learning disabilities. This approach  fosters  a  more  inclusive  and  equitable  educational  landscape,  promoting  academic  success  for  all learners.

computational advancements.

The educational trajectory is characterized by individual learning paths influenced by a myriad of factors, among which study strategies play a pivotal role. Simultaneously, a subset of students grapples with learning  disabilities,  which  can  significantly  impede  their  academic progress.  Traditional  educational  models,  while  indispensable,  often struggle to accommodate the unique cognitive profiles and requirements of  these  students.  This  calls  for  innovative  approaches  that  harness emerging AI-based technologies to bridge the gap between cognitive diversity and educational outcomes.

* Corresponding author. S ˜ ao Paulo State University, Presidente Dutra Highway, Km 137,8, CEP 12247-004, S ˜ ao Jos ´ e dos Campos - SP, Brazil. E-mail address: adriano.bressane@unesp.br (A. Bressane).





Extensive research has highlighted the importance of study strategies in shaping learning experiences and academic achievement. However, the nuanced relationship between these strategies and the presence of learning disabilities using AI tools demands further exploration. Existing studies have laid the groundwork by identifying key factors that influence academic performance, but a comprehensive understanding of how study strategies can mitigate the impact of learning disabilities remains an underexplored territory.

The current body of literature presents a conspicuous research gap pertaining to the integration of AI in analyzing the interaction between study strategies, learning disabilities, and academic performance. While studies have highlighted the potential of AI in personalized learning, its precise  role  in  optimizing  educational  approaches  for  students  with learning disabilities requires more comprehensive investigation.

This  article  aims  to  investigate  the  intricate  relationship  between study strategies, learning disabilities, and academic performance. Utilizing  AI  as  a  tool  for  informed  decision-making,  our  objective  is  to propose effective enhancements in educational approaches that facilitate  personalized  learning  experiences.  This,  in  turn,  optimizes  academic  achievement  for  students  with  diverse  cognitive  profiles  and addresses the challenges posed by learning disabilities.

Thus, this study seeks to address the aforementioned research gap by undertaking an examination of the interplay between key factors. By harnessing the power of AI algorithms, we aim to identify patterns and correlations that can inform evidence-based decision-making in educational contexts. The proposed research aspires to offer practical insights for educators, administrators, and policymakers, paving the way for the design and implementation of targeted interventions that enhance academic outcomes for all students, regardless of their cognitive profiles or learning  challenges.  Through  this  multidimensional  exploration,  our study endeavors to contribute to the advancement of educational practices that prioritize inclusivity, equity, and excellence.

To address the initial research question, this paper is organized into key sections. The first, ' Related Works, ' delves into existing research, analyzing prior studies to establish the contextual underpinning for our investigation.  Following  this,  the  section  titled ' AI-based  Decision Support System Modeling ' elucidates the methodology behind creating the model, outlining its framework and the integration of constituent elements. Subsequently, the ' Application of the Proposed Framework ' section provides practical validation through a case study, accompanied by an evaluation of the resultant proposed system. This effort allows us to  showcase  the  concrete  applicability  and  efficacy  of  the  DSS  in addressing challenges associated  with  enhancing  educational  approaches.  Lastly,  the ' Concluding Remarks ' section  encapsulates  the study ' s discoveries and their broader implications.

## 2. Related works

To  search  for  the  most  reliable,  relevant,  and  up-to-date  related works, Science Direct database were used, a premier source for scientific research. The following query was applied: title-abs-key ( ' education ' or ' study strategy ' or ' learning disability ' or ' student performance ' ) and ( ' artificial intelligence ' or ' machine learning ' or ' computational tool ' ) and (limit-to (pubstage, 'fi nal ' )) and (limit-to (doctype, ' ar ' )) and limitto (subjarea, ' edu ' )) and (limit-to (pubyear, ' from 2014 to 2023 ' )) and (limit-to  (language, ' english ' )).  This  search  yielded  204  published works, which were preliminarily screened. After excluding those outside the scope, 35 articles were selected and discussed below.

## 2.1. Inclusive education for students with learning disabilities

Students facing learning disabilities confront obstacles across various academic  domains,  encompassing  phonological  awareness,  language comprehension, time management, information processing, and problem-solving  skills.  What  study  strategies  are  most  effective  for students with learning disabilities? Previous studies suggest that students with disabilities employ diverse learning strategies, while educators  can  implement  accommodations  and  practices  to  support  their learning effectively.

Alnahdi (2015) conducted an extensive literature analysis, focusing on  instructional  strategies  targeting  improved  skills  among  students with learning disabilities. Studies were categorized into early reading methods, comprehensive approaches, and singular techniques, emphasizing the need for intensive, explicit, systematic instruction for reading skill enhancement. Maciver et al. (2018) present findings from educators identifying supportive strategies for learners with disabilities, emphasizing the role of environment and pedagogy. Bakken (2017) discusses mnemonic strategies to aid students with intellectual and developmental disabilities in memory retention.

Mori ˜ na Díez (2019) emphasizes the importance of emotions in the realm of education, particularly for students with disabilities, while also shedding light on the crucial aspect of faculty-student relationships. The study delves into the various strategies adopted by students with disabilities to facilitate their learning process, reflecting a wide range of approaches.  The  author  further  highlights  the  motivational  tactics employed  by  educators  to  cultivate  a  sense  of  inclusion,  thereby enriching  the  overall  learning  journey.  Andreou,  Athanasiadou,  and Tzivinikou (2019) discovered that accommodations such as extended time and read-aloud assistance effectively enhanced reading comprehension assessments for such students.

Mogan and Yasin (2020) engaged in a qualitative study to gather professors ' insights on selecting and implementing teaching strategies, identifying  that  multisensory  strategies,  interactive  multimedia,  and group mentoring were effective for teaching reading skills to students with  learning  disabilities.  Vats  and  Dey  (2022)  delve  into  classroom accommodation  strategies,  enhancing  disabled  students ' academic journey by cultivating empathy among educators and providing meaningful  assistance.  The  study  also  addresses  variables  influencing  accommodations and their impact on disabled students ' mental well-being.

In the face of this challenge, how can AI tools be used to help students with  learning  disabilities?  Overall,  AI  techniques  can  contribute  by replicating  human  decision-making,  minimizing  uncertainties,  and enhancing learning contexts, emphasizing their importance for intelligent and adaptable e-learning environments.

Yahya and Noor (2015) propose an e-learning DSS that estimates students ' learning  styles  automatically,  making  learning  easier  and enhancing  progress.  Colchester,  Hagras,  Alghazzawi,  and  Aldabbagh (2017)  discuss  the  use  of  AI  techniques  to  develop  accurate  student profiles and models for adaptive learning environments. Perelmutter, McGregor, and Gordon (2017) provide a systematic review of assistive technology interventions for students with learning disabilities, finding that  while  assistive  interventions  proved  overall  beneficial,  the  most effective interventions were based on word processing, multimedia, and hypertext.  Speech-to-text  systems  had  a  small  positive  effect,  while smart pens and text-to-speech systems presented mixed results.

Interactive instruction models were found to positively impact students,  promoting  educational  and  social  inclusion,  as  shown  by García-Carri ´ on (2018). In this sense, Naumova, Vytovtova, Mitiukov, and  Zulfugarzade  (2017)  developed  e-learning  methods  for  students with disabilities, and Hedin and Kann (2019) demonstrated that a course integrating  study  skills  and  reflection  seminars  enhanced  students ' ability to analyze and adjust their study habits. Cukurova, Kent, and Luckin (2019) argue that AI can support human decision-making processes  in  education  rather  than  replacing  them  through  automation. Banerjee, Lamrani, Hossain, Paudyal, and Gupta (2020) developed an AI tutor that can teach users gestures required on the field as a prerequisite, reducing resource  requirements  for  training  and  decreasing  the  time taken for making personnel job-ready.

Martiniello et al. (2021) point out that the current decade reflects on the potential of AI-based apps to facilitate learning for all post-secondary students,  including  those  with  disabilities.  According  to  August  and Tsaima (2021), AI and ML algorithms can enhance learning systems, acting as metaphorical support systems for instructors. These tools may empower  educators  to  offer  better  guidance  while  students  enjoy increased  autonomy,  supporting  automated  tutoring,  personalized learning, knowledge assessment, and task automation.

Tiwari  (2023)  highlights  the  potential  of  AI-assisted  systems  for personalized  learning,  adaptive  testing,  intelligent  tutoring  systems, learning analytics, and content creation. From a literature review, the author  explores  AI  integration  in  education,  aiming  to  enhance personalized learning experiences. Emerging areas like virtual reality education and educational game design also show promise. Overall, AI ' s potential to enhance learning is significant, but ethical considerations and continued research are crucial.

## 2.2. AI-based models for enhancing educational approaches

Kolo  and  Adepoju  (2015)  focus  on  using  a  decision  tree  (DT) approach to predict students ' academic performance, with factors such as financial status, motivation to learn, and gender found to affect performance. Thus, the study aimed to help students and teachers improve student performance and reduce the failing ratio by taking appropriate steps at the right time. However, the research only considers a limited set of factors such as financial status, motivation to learn, and gender, and does not explore other potential factors that may influence students ' academic performance.

Mesari ´ c  and ˇ Sebalj  (2016)  create  a  model  that  classifies  students based on their academic success at the end of their first year, using data on high school success, courses taken, and rank of faculty preferences. The model uses DT algorithms and identifies significant variables such as total points in the state exam, points from high school, and points in the  Croatian  language  exam.  While  the  Reduced  Error  Pruning  Tree (RepTree) algorithm achieved the highest accuracy of 79%, the J48 algorithm  demonstrated  a  higher  overall  classification  rate.  The  study highlights the importance of predicting student success in higher education and provides insights for future research. However, the authors acknowledge  that  the  model ' s  accuracy  rate  could  be  improved  by introducing additional variables, such as course grades on the first year of study or conducting primary research among the student population.

Migu ´ eis et al., 2018 propose a two-stage model that uses data mining techniques to predict the overall academic performance of university students based on their first-year academic career. It considers both the average grade achieved and the time taken to complete the degree as indicators of academic success. The study found that Random Forest (RF)  model  was  a  superior  classification  technique  compared  to  DT, Support Vector Machine (SVM), Naive Bayes, bagged trees, and boosted trees.  The  model  and  segmentation  framework  can  help  institutions promote higher performance levels and mitigate academic failure, ultimately enhancing the quality of the academic experience provided by higher education institutions.

Buena ˜ no-Fern ´ andez  et  al.,  2019  introduce  an  application  of  ML technique based on DT to predict the final grades of computer engineering students based on their historical performance of grades. The methodology involves data collection, pre-processing, grouping of students  with  similar  performance  patterns,  selection  of  a  supervised learning algorithm, and experimental process. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of the DT technique in predicting student performance. The model can be applied to support action plans to prevent dropouts, personalize  student  follow-up,  and  provide  valuable  information  to students for evaluating their academic performance. This includes taking improvement actions in subjects with a higher risk of failing.

Waheed et al. (2020) focus on using deep learning models to predict the  academic  performance  of  students  based  on  data  from  virtual learning environments (VLEs). The study uses a deep artificial neural network  on  handcrafted  features  extracted  from  clickstream  data  to predict at-risk students and provide measures for early intervention. The proposed  model  achieves  a  high  accuracy,  outperforming  logistic regression (LR) and SVM models. The study aims to assist institutes in formulating  a  framework  for  pedagogical  support  and  facilitating decision-making  in  higher  education,  but  in  addition  to  ANN-based models  being  black  boxes  with  poor  interpretability,  the  study  does not  provide  a  clear  demarcation  line  or  improved  accuracy  between quartiles for the 'distinction-pass ' category, which suggests a limitation in accurately predicting the performance of distinction students.

Cruz-Jesus et al. (2020) presents a novel approach using ML techniques  to  predict  academic  achievement  in  public  high  schools  in Portugal, comparing the performance of different AI methods. The accuracy results show that k-nearest neighbors (KNN), RF, and DT had the best performers. Artificial Neural Networks (ANN) and Extremely Randomized Trees (ERT) also achieved high accuracy, but SVM performed poorly. The paper does not explore the interpretability of these models, which could be important for making informed decisions in the education sector. The authors acknowledge that the AI models used in the study are essentially black boxes, providing no information about how they  arrived  at  their  predictions.  This  limitation  could  restrict  their widespread acceptance and adoption. Moreover, the study focuses on academic achievement in public high schools in a specific location, and don ' t provide mechanisms to adaptation of model to other contexts or conditions, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other educational contexts or countries.

L ´ azaro Alvarez, Callejas, and Griol (2020) present a case study on predicting potential  dropout in Computer Engineering in Cuba using institutional  data  and  classification  features  obtained  at  the  time  of enrollment and after the first semester and academic year. The authors tested a combination of classification features for training and testing DT (J48)  and  Multilayer  Perceptron  (MLP),  being  the  best  performance obtained with the latter. The results show that it is possible to obtain an early assessment of the risk of dropout, with high accuracy and recall for non-promoting students, which can help in defining prevention policies. The study primarily relies on decision trees and neural networks for prediction but does not explore the potential limitations or drawbacks of these specific methods in predicting student dropout.

Rodríguez-Hern ´ andez et al., 2021 tested a systematic procedure for implementing an ANN to predict academic performance in higher education and analyze the importance of various predictors of academic performance.  ANNs  outperform  other  machine-learning  algorithms. Additionally, prior academic achievement, socioeconomic conditions, and high school characteristics are identified as important predictors of academic performance in higher education.  The study  acknowledges that the predictor of prior academic achievement was operationalized only using test outcomes, and any other information on students ' grades from high school was available. This means that relevant information represented by high school grade point average could not be included in the analysis.

Riestra-Gonz ´ alez et al., 2021 analysis the early prediction of academic  performance,  with  the  objective  is  to  detect  at-risk,  fail,  and excellent students in the early stages of the course. Different classification models, including DT, Naive Bayes, LR, MLP neural network, and SVM, are created and evaluated. The accuracies of these models improve as  the  moment  of  prediction  increases,  with  MLP  achieving  the  best performance. The study focuses on solving learning management system issues and does not consider other aspects of student performance.

Mansouri,  Ravasan,  and  Ashrafi  (2021)  discuss  the  use  of  the Learning Fuzzy Cognitive Map (LFCM) approach for predicting student performance. As an advantage, the authors highlight the limitations of common prediction approaches like ANN and LR in dealing with small sample sizes, and propose the LFCM model as a practical alternative, which performs well on limited sample sizes and can be easily interpreted by humans. The research was conducted on a master ' s program, collecting longitudinal data from 30 postgraduates in four subsequent semesters, but does not explicitly provide detailed empirical findings regarding an overall accuracy of the model, focusing on each student separately to construct his/her own LFCM regarding performance antecedents and performance for three semesters, using the proposed algorithm. To such these analysis condition, the model achieved a good performance of the predicting, having in mind the small sample size. Jayagowri  and  Karpagavalli  (2021)  also  propose  the  use  of  LFCM technology to predict students ' performance. The model showed accurate predictions with small sample sizes. The authors point that LFCM model proposed in their study may require further fine-tuning of its components to improve its accuracy and effectiveness, and there may be other  factors  that  were  not  considered  in  the  model  that  could  also impact student performance.

Fig. 2. AI-based decision support system framework.



Petra and Aziz (2021) focus on analyzing student performance in higher education using fuzzy logic evaluation methods. A fuzzy inference system (FIS) based on the Mamdani technique was designed and tested on real students ' mark samples. The results of the fuzzy expert system  and  conventional  methods  were  compared  to  identify  deficiencies  in  academic  or  personality  development.  As  an  advantage highlighted by the authors, the fuzzy logic provides flexibility in allocating different weightages to each attribute based on the needs of the education institution. Thus, the proposed FIS can help in investigating and  analyzing  student  performance,  enabling  educators  to  identify deficiencies  and  advantages  of  students,  and  assist  higher  education institutions in determining appropriate lectures, activities, and assessments for students. The paper lacks information on the sample size or diversity of the students used in testing the FIS. In addition, any discussion regarding the generalizability of the findings and whether the results can be applied to different educational contexts or institutions are not presented.

Niyogisubizo, Liao, Nziyumva, Murwanashyaka, and Nshimyumukiza  (2022)  propose  a  novel  stacking  ensemble  machine  learning approach to predict student dropout in university classes based on a hybrid of RF, Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost), Gradient Boosting (GB), and Feed-forward Neural Networks (FNN). The proposed method demonstrates  greater  performance  compared  to  the  base  models,  as evaluated  by  testing  accuracy  and  the  area  under  the  curve  (AUC) metrics.  The  findings  suggest  that  the  proposed  method  can  identify students at risk of dropping out and enable early intervention to prevent dropout.  As  a  limitation,  while  the  proposed  approach  demonstrates better performance compared to the base models, the paper does not provide  a  detailed  analysis  of  the  interpretability  of  the  model.  The black-box  nature  of  some  machine  learning  algorithms  used  in  the ensemble may limit the ability to understand the underlying reasoning process and interpret the model for humans, which may harm greater acceptance and application of the proposed model by professionals in the education sector.

Fig. 3. FIS1: (a) processing disorders; (b) language disorders; (c) mental disorders (d) learning limitation; (e) decision surface.

Bressane et al. (2022) propose a fuzzy artificial intelligence-based model to forecast student performance and retention risk in engineering education. The model is built using historical records of students ' grades and retention, as well as data collected through an online survey on  learning  strategies.  By  using  data  based  on  self-reported  data collected through an online survey, which may be subject to inaccuracies,  the  main  challenge  of  the  study  was  to  propose  a  framework capable of providing accurate predictions, dealing with uncertainties inherent  in  small  data  and  subjective  data  based  on  the  student perception.  The  proposed  model  outperformed  other  AI  methods, including Cascade-Correlation Network (CCN), MLP, Polynomial Neural Network  (GMDH),  Probabilistic  Neural  Network  (PNN),  Radial  Basis Function Network (RBFN), Gene Expression Programming (GEP), Decision Tree Forest (Tree Boost), and SVM. The model ' s practical implication is  to  support  students ' planning  and  assist professors in identifying students at higher risk of retention, enabling preventive interventions. As the fuzzy model is based on a set of easily understood rules,  its  interpretability  is  a  huge  advantage  over  other  AI  models, which  are  a  real  black  box,  so  the  proposed  model  should  achieve greater acceptance by education professionals. Nevertheless, the proposal does not address factors beyond academic performance that may contribute to students ' performance, such as health conditions or learning disabilities.

Priyambada  et  al.  (2023)  propose  a  two-layer  ensemble  learning technique that combines ensemble learning and ensemble-based progressive prediction to predict students ' performance using their learning behavior and domain knowledge. The proposal showed improved accuracy on a real-world student dataset, outperforming SVM, KNN, and RF models. The authors argue that proposed framework can assist academic  stakeholders  in  taking  actions  to  ensure  timely  graduation  of students  and  provide  valuable  information  for  students  to  make informed decisions about their academic paths. However, the framework does not encompass elements beyond academic achievement that could potentially influence students ' outcomes, such as health conditions or impediments to learning.

Han (2023) focuses on the analysis of college students ' stress levels, psychological well-being, and academic performance using the Heuristic Fuzzy C-means Clustering Algorithm (HFCA). The study aims to identify the most crucial aspects of student success, such as student involvement and  satisfaction.  The  proposed  HFCA  method  achieves  high  performance  in  the  prediction  ratios  compared  to  other  methods,  but  the model has limitations in  performing with  a limited-size  dataset.  The paper also emphasizes the importance of understanding risk factors for poor mental well-being and developing targeted interventions to prevent health problems among college students.

## 2.3. Research gap

The existing literature, as demonstrated through the comprehensive review of related works, has shed light on various facets of inclusive education,  student  performance  prediction,  and  the  potential  of  AIdriven tools. However, despite the advances, there remains a research gap  that  calls  for  a  more  holistic  and  integrated  exploration  of  the intricate relationship between study strategies, learning disabilities, and their  collective  impact  on  academic  performance.  This  research  gap underscores the need for a study that bridges the existing knowledge and delves deeper into the interplay of these factors to propose practical solutions for enhancing the educational approach.

While previous studies have individually examined study strategies, inclusive education practices, and AI ' s potential in education, there is a dearth  of  research  that  synthesizes  these  elements  into  a  unified framework.  Moreover,  the  influence  of  learning  disabilities  on  the effectiveness of study strategies and the subsequent academic performance is a relatively underexplored area. Such an investigation holds substantial  significance  given  the  increasing  diversity  of  learners  in educational  settings  and  the  evolving  role  of  AI  in  educational technologies.

Thus, the present study aims to address this research gap, with a special focus on the potential of AI-driven tools to enhance these interactions. By integrating insights from multiple disciplines including education,  psychology,  and  AI,  this  study  seeks  to  provide  a  more nuanced and complete perspective on how students with learning disabilities engage with study strategies and how these interactions influence their academic outcomes.

Therefore, the motivation for conducting this study is multifaceted. First,  it  aligns  with  the  current  trends  in  educational  research  and practice that emphasize the importance of personalized learning experiences for all students, including those with learning disabilities. Second, the integration of AI tools into education is a burgeoning field, and there is a growing need to explore how these tools can be harnessed to provide meaningful support for students with diverse learning needs. Third,  the  study  can  contribute  to  the  development  of  practical  interventions and decision support systems that educators and institutions can utilize to enhance their inclusive educational practices.

In  summary,  the  identified  research  gap,  which  stems  from  the limited integration of study strategies, learning disabilities, and AI in educational contexts, justifies the need for a comprehensive study that investigates these factors collectively. This study can provide valuable insights for educators, researchers, and policymakers aiming to create inclusive and effective learning environments for all students. Furthermore, the proposed decision support system has the potential to offer actionable  recommendations,  bridging  theory  and  practice  for  the betterment of education.

## 3. AI -based decision support system modeling

The methodology employed in crafting a DSS to enhance education approach included the use of AI algorithms based on ANN and fuzzy inference system (FIS). To do this, an empirical dataset needs to cover key factors that influence students ' academic performance, as demonstrated later, through a case study.

The  collected  data  underwent  thorough  preprocessing  to  ensure uniformity  and  integrity.  The  data  should  be  then  structured  into appropriate features, such as the frequency of specific study strategies, and indicators of how much the existence of learning disabilities hinders performance, and the final course grades [0, 10] achieved by the respondents. The study strategies are organized into three categories: 'selfregulation strategies, ' 'cognitive strategies, ' and 'emotional strategies. '

strategies

Fig. 5. FIS3: (a) learning limitation; (b) learning potential; (c) educational recommendation.



Within these categories, specific study strategies have been classified based on their characteristics and intended focus.

In  this  sense,  the  self-regulation  strategies  encompass  'Studying before  class ' ,  'Reviewing  material  prior  to  assessments ' ,  'Consistent material review ' , 'Regular attendance in class ' , 'Seeking assistance when needed ' , 'Collaborative group study ' , 'Sustained attention during study sessions ' , 'Increased effort when encountering challenges ' . In turn, the cognitive  strategies  includes  'Taking  classroom  notes ' ,  'Summarizing lectures ' ,  'Engaging in active reading ' ,  'Creating reading summaries ' , 'Solving exercises for practice ' ,  'Generating questions to enhance understanding ' ,  'Constructing diagrams for visualization ' ,  'Relating new knowledge  to  prior  information ' ,  'Mentally  rehearsing  concepts  for reinforcement ' , 'Teaching others to solidify comprehension ' , 'Employing mnemonic techniques for memory enhancement ' ,  'Utilizing audio recordings for review ' , 'Employing flashcards for quick recall ' . Finally, the emotional strategies comprise 'Maintaining calmness when facing difficulties ' ,  'Sustaining  composure  despite  the  possibility  of  subpar performance ' , 'Staying composed when making errors during exercises ' , 'Engaging in relaxation techniques for emotional equilibrium ' , 'Seeking motivation  to  combat  waning  interest ' ,  'Reflecting  on  the  broader relevance of content to other subjects ' , 'Contemplating the significance of content for one ' s future profession ' .

Similarly, the learning disabilities are classified into three distinct groups: 'processing disorders ' , 'language disorders ' , and 'mental disorders ' .  This  categorization  aims  to  capture  the  varied  dimensions  of challenges that learners might encounter in their educational journey. The  processing  disorders  encompass  'Phonological  dyslexia ' ,  'Dyscalculia ' ,  and  'Ageometric ' difficulties.  The  language  disorders  include 'Visual  dyslexia ' ,  'Dysgraphia ' ,  and  'Dysnomia.  The  mental  disorders comprise 'Anxiety ' , ' Stress ' , and 'Depression ' .

Then, a deep learning MLP-based neural network is modeled with this preprocessed empirical dataset. Through 5-fold cross-validation, the MLP learns to recognize patterns related to associations between key factors (Fig. 1).

The ANN modeling can be performed using the Orange Data Mining software (Demsar et al., 2013), a platform that offers a range of tools for analyzing  and  modeling  complex  datasets.  Specifically,  the  'Explain ' functionality  within  the  software  is  crucial  in  the  process  of  feature weighting  by  the  ANN.  This  weighting  is  guided  by  the  variables ' contribution to the coefficient of determination (R 2 ), an indicator of the model ' s ability to explain the variance observed in the dependent variable. By assessing the influence of each variable on the R 2 value, the AI can discern the most influential factors driving academic performance.

Building upon the pondered features by the ANN, a model grounded in fuzzy logic is developed to serve as the DSS for recommendations for effective educational interventions geared toward enhancing students ' academic performance. The choice to employ a fuzzy logic-based model derives  from  its  innate  capability  to  navigate  and  accommodate  the inherent uncertainty intrinsic to subjective data, in this case, data based on respondents ' perceptions (Bressane, Biagolini, Mochizuki, Roveda, &amp; Lourenço, 2017; Bressane, Mochizuki, Caram, &amp; Roveda, 2016). Fuzzy logic is particularly apt for processing data that embodies varying degrees of certainty or vagueness, which are commonplace when dealing with human-centric inputs (Bressane, Silva, P.M., Fiore, Carra, Ewbank, &amp; De-Carli, 2020).

In the context of our study, the data obtained from the self-reporting forms  carries  a  level  of  subjectivity  inherent  to  the  respondents ' interpretations and self-assessments. Thus, fuzzy logic enabled the incorporation of subjectivity into the decision-making process, allowing us to effectively model the nuanced and diverse factors influencing academic performance. By adopting fuzzy logic, the DSS is equipped to represent the complexity of the cognitive strategies ' effectiveness and the presence of learning disabilities as gradual and imprecise values. This flexibility enables  the  DSS  to  handle  cases  where  the  boundary  between  the effectiveness of strategies or the certainty of the presence of a learning disorder may not be clearly delineated. Fuzzy sets and fuzzy rules enable the incorporation of linguistic variables and the incorporation of qualitative  insights  into  the  decision-making  framework  (Bressane  et  al., 2023a,b; Loureiro, Bressane, Nascimento, Sim ˜ oes, &amp; Negri, 2023). As such,  the  fuzzy  logic-based  model  integrates  both  quantitative  and qualitative data, allowing us to use the subjective perspectives of the respondents. This holistic approach empowers educators and administrators to make well-rounded decisions that account for the diversity of student experiences and perceptions. This proposed framework can be seen in Fig. 2.

The FIS is built by integrating two preceding subsystems. The first Educational approach recommendations based on FIS3.

one (FIS1) is dedicated to analyzing the variables 'processing disorders, ' 'language disorders, ' and 'mental disorders. ' The output is the level of 'learning limitation, ' categorized as very small, small, moderate, large, and very large (Fig. 3).

In this process, linguistic labels (low, medium, and high) are assigned to the input variables. Guided by a rule basis (Table 1), the degrees of membership for these labels are then utilized in the inference process to derive an assessment of the student ' s learning limitation.

Likewise, FIS2 follows a parallel structure, incorporating the input variables 'self-regulation strategies, ' 'cognitive strategies, ' and 'emotional strategies ' (Fig. 4). The result of this integration provides a comprehensive assessment of 'learning potential.

During this inference process, governed by the rule basis (Table 2), each  input  variable  undergoes  a  parallel  membership  assignment  to linguistic labels, quantifying their individual contributions to the overall learning potential. The resultant 'learning potential ' is then categorized into one of the predefined linguistic labels, aiding informed decisionmaking and the development of tailored educational strategies based on the specifics of the assessed potential.

The outputs from the two preceding subsystems are brought together in the third and final system (FIS3), guided by the rule base shown in Table 3. This integration culminates in the generation of educational approach recommendations that holistically factor in all the previously analyzed  variables,  shaping  a  comprehensive  outlook  on  students ' learning profiles (Fig. 5).

Upon obtaining the defuzzied FIS3 outputs in crisp real values, this integration  resulted  in  a  DSS  for  intervention  actions  that  aim  at improving the educational approach, including recommendations for teaching techniques and assessment methods that are most appropriate for each learning condition (limitation and potential), as can be seen in Table 4.

By combining the insights from these two critical dimensions -learning limitations and potential -the FIS3 provides a comprehensive  understanding  of  students ' individual  profiles,  which allows crafting educational approaches aligned with the multifaceted characteristics exhibited by each student, transcending a one-size-fits-all approach.

## 4. Application of the proposed framework

In this case study, we delve into the practical implementation of the Fuzzy  DSS.  The  empirical  dataset  was  sourced  from  an  engineering undergraduate population. The data collection was carried out using an online  self-reporting  form,  which  encompassed  the  study  strategies, learning disabilities, and academic performance indicators. The survey link  was distributed through official university communication channels,  fostering  a  representative  sample,  providing  a  total  of  822  responses. The process adhered to ethical considerations, with participants providing  informed  consent  (Table  5).  Fig.  6  shows  how  much  the learning disabilities hinders the student ' s performance.

Fig. 7 depicts the density of employing study strategies, which illustrates the extent to which students utilize these strategies in their learning  endeavors,  providing  insights  into  the  patterns  of  strategy adoption and their potential impact on academic performance.

By using this dataset (Figs. 6 and 7), the ANN model with the highest accuracy during machine learning was attained through a tuning process. This configuration entailed the utilization of 200 neurons distributed  across  hidden  layers.  The  logistic  function  was  selected  as  the activation function, to boost the model ' s ability to model complex relationships within the variables. The L-BFGS-B solver was employed for optimization, effectively navigating the model toward a convergence point that minimized error. Regularization was implemented to prevent overfitting, with an alpha value set to 7.10 4 . With a commitment to model convergence and stability, a maximum of 300 iterations was set for the training process. This ensemble of parameters culminated in a MLP that achieved an impressive R 2 value of 0.938, indicating a strong fit between the model ' s predictions and the observed academic performance. Fig. 8 shows the decrease in the R 2 associated with the removal of each variable, indicating the most influential ones driving academic performance and their relative weight ( w ). Before entering the FIS, the values declared in the form by the student regarding the frequency of study  strategies  (Fig.  7)  and  the  occurrence  of  learning  disabilities (Fig. 6) are first pondered by these weights (Fig. 8).

Fig. 6. Occurrence of learning disabilities: 0 -never, 1 -rarely, 2 -often, and 3 -always.



Through this process, the proposed AI-based framework is fine-tuned on  a  case-by-case  basis  to  the  specific  characteristics  of  the  student cohort for which the educational approach will be recommended. This calibration is rooted in recognized patterns through the analysis of the historical  empirical  database,  as  well  as  the  distinct  profile  of  each student, particularly in terms of the study strategies they consistently employ.

Illustrating with an example based on a respondent ' s answers from the survey, let ' s consider the following declared values for the frequency at which learning disabilities negatively impact academic performance: phonological dyslexia - never (0), dyscalculia - often (2), ageometric difficulties  -  always  (3).  These  values  sum  up  to  a  total  of  5  for  the processing disorders. However, when incorporating the weights determined  by  the  ANN,  the  weighted  value  for  Dyscalculia  would  be  2 (declared value) multiplied by 0.415 (weight assigned to this variable), and for Ageometric difficulties, the weighted value would be 3 multiplied by 0.424. This would result in a cumulative weighted value of 2.102 for the Processing Disorders. This process is repeated for all input variables within the FIS. Each input value provided by the respondent is adjusted  based  on  the  respective  weights  assigned  by  the  neural network. For this same respondent, the weighted values of the variables led FIS1 to yield a 'moderate ' level of learning limitation, while FIS2 indicated  a  'large ' learning  potential.  The  integrated  analysis  within FIS3 consequently resulted in the recommendation group labeled as '8. ' In essence, for this particular student, the optimal educational approach would involve fostering a collaborative learning environment enriched with opportunities for peer mentoring.

In terms of teaching techniques, the recommendation suggests the utilization of technology-enhanced learning platforms along with group problem-solving activities. These methods aim to foster active engagement and collaborative learning experiences, aligning with the student ' s recognized  learning  potential  and  preferences.  Furthermore,  the  recommended assessment methods for this student align with the identified learning potential and strategies. These assessment strategies combine the use of quizzes, debates, and collaborative presentations. By assessing knowledge application through these diverse methods, educators can better capture the student ' s comprehensive understanding of the material and their ability to apply it in various contexts.

In  sum,  the  intricate  interplay  between  the  three  FIS  enables  the generation  of  a  tailored  educational  approach  for  this  student.  This approach aligns with the student ' s learning limitations, potential, and study  strategies,  encompassing  collaborative  learning  environments, technology-enhanced methods, and diversified assessment techniques. This  individualized  recommendation  underscores  the  study ' s  overarching goal of providing precise and effective educational strategies that cater to the unique learning profiles of each student.

Applying the proposed DSS to a cohort of 35 first-year students in an incoming class yielded distinct sets of recommendations from FIS3: set '5 ' was recommended to 19 students, set '7 ' to 7 students, set '4 ' to 7 students, and set '2 ' to 2 students. This stratification ensures that students  with  similar  learning  profiles  are  grouped  together  in  class, facilitating  the  implementation  of  focused  and  targeted  educational strategies. By categorizing students based on their recommendation sets, educators can streamline their approach, tailoring their teaching techniques, resources, and assessments to the specific needs and preferences of each group. This approach can optimize the learning experience for each student and maximize the potential for academic success within the class.

## 5. Concluding remarks

Returning to the initial research question, our results allow us to conclude that AI-integrated study strategies can mitigate the impact of learning disabilities on academic performance by providing personalized interventions, adapting educational approaches to students ' cognitive profiles, and fostering inclusivity, resulting in improved academic  outcomes.  The  existing  landscape  of  traditional  educational methods often falls short in accommodating the diverse needs of students,  particularly  those  with  learning  disabilities.  This  research  endeavors to bridge this gap by providing a comprehensive understanding of how study strategies and AI-based DSS can work synergistically to optimize educational outcomes. By employing the proposed AI

framework,  educators  can  make  informed  decisions  to  customize educational approaches that align with the unique cognitive profiles of students.  Through  personalized  interventions  guided  by  recognized patterns, the academic journey becomes more inclusive and effective, benefiting  all  learners  regardless  of  their  cognitive  backgrounds  or learning challenges.

The practical implications of this research are significant for educators,  policymakers,  and  administrators.  The  proposed  data-driven strategies  offer  a  blueprint  for  enhancing  teaching  methodologies, fostering an inclusive learning environment, and promoting academic success for students with learning disabilities. By embracing personalized interventions based on students ' learning limitations and potential, educational institutions can foster a more equitable educational landscape,  where  individual  strengths  are  nurtured,  and  challenges  are addressed effectively.

In  a  rapidly  evolving  educational  landscape,  where  diversity  and inclusivity are paramount, this study ' s insights and methodologies pave the  way  for  transformative  approaches  to  teaching  and  learning.  By harnessing the power of AI and leveraging the nuanced relationships between  study  strategies,  learning  disabilities,  and  academic  performance, educators can embark on a journey of continuous improvement, ensuring that each student ' s learning experience is optimized for success.  Ultimately,  this  research  contributes  to  the  advancement  of educational practices that prioritize individual growth, inclusivity, and excellence in the pursuit of academic achievement. For future studies, there  are  some  directions  that  can  further  expand  and  improve  the proposed framework. Comparing the effectiveness of traditional teaching methods with the AI-driven educational recommendations can be carried out through longitudinal studies, providing insights into how study strategies evolve over time and their impact on academic performance, especially for students with learning disabilities. By delving into this  direction,  future  research  endeavors  can  contribute  to  a  deeper understanding of how study strategies and learning disabilities intersect, and how AI-based tools can be harnessed to create more effective, inclusive, and personalized educational experiences for all students.
